[get_page_text] Title: LSATU
URL: https://lsatprep.study
Source element: <main>
---
PRACTICE · REVIEW · COACHING
LSATPREP.STUDY
FOR ASPIRING ATTORNEYS
Master the LSAT. Own your future.
Master the LSAT.
Own your future.

The discipline is ancient. The training is new.

Enter LSAT University
Or create an account

EMAIL OR GOOGLE · NO CREDIT CARD TO START

DESCEND
THE TWELVE MOVES
Every question is one of twelve moves.
Learn to see the move.

Twelve question types cover everything Logical Reasoning can throw at you. We drill them until you recognize the move before you finish the stem.

READING — / XII
I
Necessary Assumption
THE UNSTATED BRIDGE
II
Sufficient Assumption
THE MISSING GUARANTEE
III
Strengthen
REINFORCE THE GAP
IV
Weaken
ATTACK THE GAP
V
Flaw in the Reasoning
NAME THE BREAK
VI
Main Conclusion
FIND THE POINT
VII
Method of Reasoning
DESCRIBE THE MOVES
VIII
Role in Argument
PLACE THE STATEMENT
IX
Principle
MATCH THE RULE
X
Parallel Reasoning
MATCH THE SHAPE
XI
Inference
WHAT MUST BE TRUE
XII
Resolve the Paradox
RECONCILE THE FACTS
THE METHOD
Thought, taking form.

You are not memorizing tricks. You are training a way of seeing arguments, until the structure appears the moment you start to read.

SPEC · POINT CLOUD
SURFACE · SAMPLED
FIG. 01 · ASSEMBLY RIDES YOUR SCROLL · THE CLOUD ANSWERS YOUR CURSOR
THE CANON
An argument has proportions.

A premise carries a conclusion the way a plinth carries a figure: with a measurable relationship between them. Once you can see that geometry, you can see where it breaks. That is the whole test.

GIVEN · DRAWN · MEASURED
PREMISE
CONCLUSION
1 : 1.618
INFERENCE
DISCIPLINE I
Smart LR Drill

The drill reads your misses and serves the question that moves your score next. No busywork, no random sets.

INPUT · YOUR MISSES / OUTPUT · YOUR NEXT QUESTION
YOUR MISSES
NEXT
ADAPTIVE SELECTION
DISCIPLINE II
Wrong Answer Journal

Every miss becomes a worked example: why the trap worked, what the right read was, and the pattern to catch next time.

THE TRAP · THE RIGHT READ · THE PATTERN
THE TRAP
THE RIGHT READ
ONE MISS · ONE LESSON
DISCIPLINE III
Guided bootcamps

Structured, voice-led courses that build one reasoning skill at a time, starting where the points are.

MAIN CONCLUSION FIRST · THEN THE WHOLE FAMILY
ENTER
ONE SKILL AT A TIME
DISCIPLINE IV
A schedule that adapts

A week-by-week plan built around your test date and your data, so the next right thing to do is always obvious.

YOUR TEST DATE · YOUR DATA · YOUR WEEK
WEEK ONE
TEST DAY
TUNED TO YOUR TEST DATE
WEIGHED DAILY
Judgment is a habit. We train it every day.
Judgment is a habit.
We train it every day.
Reason

Evidence

Structure

Judgment

A STATEMENT OF INTENT

“LSAT U is the prep we wish every aspiring attorney had: practice, review, and real progress in one place, all pointed at the score you actually want.”

Begin training
THE ACADEMY IS OPEN
Sit down.
Begin.
Enter LSAT University

---
name: grilling
description: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.
disable-model-invocation: true
---

Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.

Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven't heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.

Format a round like so:

```
❓ **Q1** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>

➡️ <your recommended answer>

---

❓ **Q2** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>

➡️ <your recommended answer>
```

Each round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.

Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, etc.), dispatch a sub-agent to find it; don't ask the user for anything you could look up yourself. Don't block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait for the sub-agent to report; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.

The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch of the design tree visited, nothing left silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

< description: A relentless interview to sharpen a plan, design, or topic, checkpointing every answer to a brainstorm file so nothing is lost. Use when the user wants to stress-test a plan, get grilled on a design, run a brainstorm or discovery session, extract what's in their head into a doc, build up their AI OS context over time, or says "grill me".
---
> description: A relentless interview to sharpen a plan or design.
> disable-model-invocation: true
6,87c7
< # Grill Me
< 
< Two layers. The **interview method** comes from the `grilling` skill and is not restated here. The **capture discipline** below is what this wrapper adds: every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up.
< 
< If the topic is a feature, component, or other implementation work rather than an open-ended plan or decision, `brainstorming` runs a similar interview but gates on a written, approved design doc before any code — reach for that instead when the destination is implementation.
< 
< ## Step 1: set up the capture file (BEFORE the first question)
< 
< 1. Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`; in an established project follow its declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.
< 2. Create the capture file at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create `brainstorms/` if missing). Every capture lives here, one predictable home regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. A polished deliverable (plan, map, spec) may later move into the relevant `projects/` folder; the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
<    - Get today's real date from the clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
<    - Never overwrite an existing capture. Same date and topic: add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user asks or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.

import io
paths=[r"C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\grill-me\SKILL.md", r"C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\skills\claude\grill-me\SKILL.md"]
old_intro = "Two layers. The **interview method** comes from the `grilling` skill and is not restated here. The **capture discipline** below is what this wrapper adds: every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up."
new_intro = "One skill, self-contained. It holds both the **interview method** (Step 2) and the **capture discipline** (Steps 1 and 3): every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up. It does not call the vendor `grilling` skill; that skill is `disable-model-invocation` and cannot be invoked from here. Its method is written inline below instead."
start = "## Step 2: run the interview"
end = "## Step 3"
method = '''## Step 2: run the interview

Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.

Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you have not heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.

Format a round like so:

```
**Q1 - <question title>**: <question body, may be multiple paragraphs, including choices>

Recommended: <your recommended answer>

---

**Q2 - <question title>**: ...

Recommended: ...
```

Each round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.

Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, live site), look it up or dispatch a sub-agent; never ask the user for anything you could find yourself. Do not block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.

The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch visited, nothing silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

Two adjustments the capture layer imposes on the method:

- For **decisions**, give a recommended answer grounded in context and label it as a recommendation. For **personal or business facts**, ask neutrally. Never lead with invented facts, and never save your inference as the user's answer.
- When the user **cannot answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Do not stall the round on it.

'''
for p in paths:
    s=io.open(p,encoding="utf-8").read()
    assert old_intro in s, p
    s=s.replace(old_intro,new_intro)
    i=s.index(start); j=s.index(end)
    s=s[:i]+method+s[j:]
    s=s.replace("As `grilling` requires: do not act","Do not act")
    io.open(p,"w",encoding="utf-8",newline="\n").write(s)
    print("ok",p, "grilling" in s)
EOF

**Round 1**

**Q1 - Whose Home comes first**: Students and you see different versions of this page. Which one are we designing for first?

Recommended: the student Home. It's what paying students open every day. Your teacher view can follow the same design afterward.

---

**Q2 - The feeling**: When a student opens Home, what should they feel? For example "calm and focused", "a premium private study room", or "momentum, I'm getting better". Pick one of those or say it your way.

Recommended: a premium private study room. It fits the warm serif look you already have and matches the one-to-one coaching brand.

---

**Q3 - The empty right side on desktop**: Keep one centered column, or add a second column?

Recommended: add a right column with progress, such as score trend, streak and weakest question type, next to the main to-do column. That turns empty space into a reason to come back.

---

**Q4 - Visual only, or new content too**: Only restyle what's there (greeting, message, section card, session card, week strip), or also add new blocks like progress, streaks or the next recommended drill?

Recommended: both. The page looks thin mostly because it has little on it, not because of how it's styled.

---

**Q5 - Motion**: The page already has drifting glows and a shine sweep. More motion, the same amount, or calmer?

Recommended: about the same amount, but used on purpose. Numbers count up when the page loads, cards settle in, and one highlight lands on today's task. No new background effects.

---

**Q6 - The sideways scrollbar**: The background glows spill past the right edge and add a scrollbar at the bottom. Fix it now as a small separate change, or fold it into the redesign?

Recommended: fix it now. It's a small CSS change and it's visible to every student today.

/* ── Classroom Home ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   The student dashboard. Warm paper: the rail's gradient no longer stops at
   its own edge, it becomes the page. Hierarchy is the point — one primary
   action, then the work, then the coach and the next session, then the week.

   The Classroom's warm palette is a deliberate, scoped exception to the app's
   monochrome editorial system. Values here are the design handoff's, carried
   as tokens rather than repeated inline.

   Every hairline is a box-shadow ring, never a border: borders take layout
   space and shift a card by a pixel when a hover state thickens them.
   Every animation is transform/opacity/box-shadow only, so nothing on this
   page can reflow while it moves. */

## assistant

I built a first master design in Claude Design: https://claude.ai/artifact/6zF3FgewiMA7pCZSk766Xt

I haven't opened it to check how it renders; the canvas instructions say not to check unless you ask, so look it over or tell me to check it.

**What's on the canvas:**
- **What each reference gives us:** one card per screenshot. Each says what it looks like, what we take from it, and where that shows up in the master.
- **Master Home:** the student view, built in your current cream, orange and dark-brown colors and your current fonts (Fraunces and Outfit). Top to bottom:
  - **Top bar:** search, plus two small rounded tags for days to test and study streak (from Coders).
  - **Hero:** the greeting, a row of four big numbers and a goal dial showing how far they are toward their target score (from Homies Lab and WeatherWise).
  - **Three cards:**
    - **Up next:** the section due today, with a progress bar and a marker (from Be.run).
    - **Practice test trend:** a line of practice test scores with a glowing point on the latest one and the goal marked (from Homies Lab and WeatherWise).
    - **Training days:** a dark month calendar showing days studied and session days (from Be.run).
  - **Where the points are:** one row per question type with a segmented bar, weakest first, linking to Smart Drill (from Be.run).
  - **Beside it:** your latest message, and a black "Next session" card with Join and Reschedule buttons (from Coders).
  - **Next seven days:** drawn as a curve with a glowing dot on today (from WeatherWise).

Every number is a sample in [brackets]. The Home page gets no scores, accuracy or streak data today. That data sits on the Analytics page, and some of it may not be tracked at all. So each number we keep needs a real source before it goes live.

Notton and the mountain dashboard gave us almost nothing directly. I took the equal-height card row and the soft fill under the chart line from them and left out the big photos. Say so if you want a photo band.

Everything is saved to the brainstorm file.

**Round 2** (the questions from round 1 you haven't answered yet are folded in here)

**Q1 - Direction**: Is the master the right direction: light and warm, with two dark cards (calendar and next session) to anchor it? Or should the whole page go dark like Notton and WeatherWise?

Recommended: stay light with the dark cards. It keeps the study-room warmth, and the dark cards give it depth.

---

**Q2 - Which numbers go on Home**: The master shows latest PT score, LR accuracy over 14 days, questions this week, sessions left on the pass, a goal dial, a streak and days to test. Which of these do you actually want students to see?

Recommended: keep latest PT, questions this week, sessions left, days to test and the goal dial. Cut the streak unless you want to push daily habits. Accuracy invites anxiety on a bad week.

---

**Q3 - The weakest question types**: Should Home show a student their weakest question types by name?

Recommended: yes. It turns the page into "here's where your points are" and leads straight into Smart Drill. That's the teaching brand.

---

**Q4 - Your view**: Build the student Home first and give your teacher view the same design afterward?

Recommended: yes.

---

**Q5 - Motion**: Numbers count up on load, cards settle in, and the glowing dots pulse gently. Nothing more?

Recommended: yes, and turn it all off for anyone whose system asks for reduced motion.

---

**Q6 - The sideways scrollbar**: Fix it now as a separate small change on its own branch?

Recommended: yes.

CONTEXT-MAP.md
# Public Surface — Context

The unauthenticated landing + sign-in page (`Auth.tsx`): how LSAT U presents itself to a prospective student before they have an account. This file is the glossary for that marketing copy, so the language stays consistent and on-voice. It is not a spec.

## Voice

Student-facing copy here follows the **warm coach** voice (canonical in the vault: `wiki/concepts/teaching-voice.md`): encouraging, concrete, second-person, calm not hyped, no gotcha energy. Two surface-specific rules:

- **Platform, not a person.** The voice is LSAT U speaking as a platform ("we", "you"), never an individual founder ("I", "my", "Joshua's"). The product is the coach; no single person narrates.
- **No em dashes.** Ever (a standing global rule). Use the middot `·` as the separator, matching the rest of the page.

## Language

**Habit**:
One of the six trained reading behaviors the landing page showcases (Anatomy, Bridge, Failure, Stems, Patterns, Pace). The canonical word for these.
_Avoid_: Principle (collides with the question type, see below), card (never name the UI element in prose; describe the habit, not the card it sits on).

**Drill**:
The act of practicing a habit through repetition. The thing a student "sits down to do." A verb and the unit of practice.

**Principle**:
**Reserved for the LSAT Logical Reasoning question type only** (it appears in `QUESTION_TYPES`). Do NOT use "principle" to mean a training habit or a reasoning pattern. That overload is the bug this glossary fixes.

**Pattern**:
A recurring reasoning structure that repeats across many questions (the "Hunt patterns" habit). Distinct from Principle the question type.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

[external_agent_tool_result]
# Context Map

The repo has more than one bounded context, each with its own language. This map points to where each glossary lives.

## Contexts

- [Public surface](./src/pages/CONTEXT.md) — the unauthenticated landing + sign-in page (`Auth.tsx`): how LSAT U describes itself to a prospective student.
- [Teacher workspace](./src/pages/teacher/CONTEXT.md) — Joshua's admin operator surface (`/teacher`): assigning prepared material to students and the transcript pipeline.
- [Teacher Desk page](./src/pages/teacher/desk/CONTEXT.md) — the named visual regions of the desk page itself (the boxes on screen), nested under the teacher workspace. Names the layout so we can revise it precisely.
- [Guided Drills & Explanations](./src/components/drill/CONTEXT.md) — the practice domain: guided drills, the question bank, drill instruction cards, and per-question explanations (the Phase 2 authoring work).
- [Classroom](./src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md) — the student's own room: the Classroom frame, its sections, and the flag-versus-wrong-answer distinction.
- [Reading Comprehension](./src/features/rc/CONTEXT.md) — full-passage RC drilling: passages, sessions, attempts, reveal modes, status, and the adaptive picker.
- [Activity Centre](./src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md) — coach-led skill exercises a student is assigned, and the Read, Recall, Colour drill that runs inside one: Chunk, Detail, Coverage, The Recall, Report Card.
- [Booking](./src/components/booking/CONTEXT.md) — paying for and scheduling tutoring time: Packages, Booking Passes, the public microsite, and the Single Session (Slots, Holds, Bookers).

## Relationships

- Both surfaces describe the same product but to different audiences (prospective student vs. operator), so a word can carry different weight in each. Check the right glossary before reusing a term.
- A **flag** (Classroom) and a **wrong answer** (Wrong Answer Journal) are different things on different tables: a flag is chosen mid-question, a wrong answer is earned by missing one. Never collapse the two, and never let "flagged" drift into meaning "struggled with".
- **Set** now means one thing everywhere: the group a student assembles and drills. It used to mean a passage's position within its section in Reading Comprehension; that sense is now called **Position** (the data still carries `setNumber`). The collision was retired when the LR and RC pickers merged into one surface — a primary noun that changed meaning when you flipped the mode was the confusion the merge existed to remove. See `docs/adr/0006-rc-selection-is-question-grain.md`.
7:**Classroom frame** — the persistent layout every Classroom route is drawn in: the cream left rail, an optional middle list, and the white reading surface. Implemented by `ClassroomLayout`. Navigating between surfaces it draws swaps the content only and never remounts the rail. A page "sits in the Classroom frame" regardless of its URL; `/flagged` and `/journal` both do.
9:**StudentShell** — the older, separate frame with its own 240px rail, still drawing `/mainhub`, `/practice` and the rest. A student moving between a Classroom-frame page and a StudentShell page sees the whole page rebuild. Reducing that boundary is ongoing work, one surface at a time.
11:**Reading column** — the white content column. Most routes use one reading column on the right. A Wrong answers detail uses a four-column Classroom frame: cream rail, entries list, vertical question, and review panel. Below its journal-only breakpoints the entries list becomes an overlay and the review panel moves under the question.
13:**Drill landing workspace** — the pre-question surface at `/classroom/drill`, drawn inside the Classroom frame. It includes drill selection, launch controls, practice insights, and today's work; it excludes the official question-answering experience.
16:**Question finder** — the one control that turns something a student can say about a question into that question ...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

**Where we are**

1. You asked for a better Classroom Home, the first page a student sees after sign-in.
2. I looked at the live Classroom Home. It is one narrow column with a lot of empty space. It has one bug: the background glows make a sideways scrollbar.
3. You said: do not use anything from the Public surface (the landing and sign-in page).
4. I fixed the `grill-me` skill. It now runs the interview itself. It does not need the `grilling` skill.
5. You sent six dashboard pictures. I took a small number of parts from each picture.
6. I made one design, the "master". It is on a canvas at https://claude.ai/artifact/6zF3FgewiMA7pCZSk766Xt. It is only a picture. I did not change code.

**What the master shows, from top to bottom**

1. The Question finder, the days to the test, and the streak.
2. The greeting, four large numbers, and a dial that shows progress to the goal score.
3. Three cards:
   - The next Assigned work, with a progress bar.
   - The line of practice test scores.
   - A dark calendar of study days.
4. The weakest question types, with a link to Smart Drill.
5. The latest coach message, and the next session in a black card.
6. The next seven days, shown as a curve.

**One problem**

The numbers in the master are examples, not real data. Classroom Home does not get scores, accuracy, or streaks at this time. Some of that data is on the Analytics page. Some of it is not tracked yet.

**One more problem I found now**

The Classroom glossary says: "Ask" must not show the coach's name. My master shows "From Joshua" on the message card. On the live page, that label shows the student's name. That label is on the message itself, so it is not an "Ask" control, but I will check it before we build.

**What I need from you**

1. Keep the page light with two dark cards, or make all of the page dark?
2. Which numbers do you want to show? Real data must be found for each one.
3. Show each student their weakest question types by name?
4. Design the student view first, then the teacher view?
5. Small movement only: numbers count up and cards move in. Stop all movement when the student's device asks for reduced motion?
6. Fix the sideways scrollbar now, as a separate change?

For each question, my recommendation is "yes". For question 2, I recommend: latest practice test score, questions this week, sessions left, days to the test, and the goal dial.

<result>Five of the seven items already have real data. The practice test (PT) score history is the main gap, and the starting and goal scores exist only as score ranges, not exact numbers.

**1. Latest PT score and trend: PARTIAL**
- The `practice_test_runs` table has `pt`, `scaled_score`, `score_source`, `completed_at` and `status` (types.ts:3126, migration `20260829120000_practice_test_runs.sql:93`). No code in `src/` reads or writes it.
- The app saves in-app runs to localStorage only (`src/lib/practiceTest.ts:340`, `listPracticeTestRuns` at :361). Score is estimated from raw correct by `estimateScaledScore` (:279).
- Scores imported from other platforms are in `student_external_metrics` where `metric_kind='pt_score'`. Read them with `useStudentExternalMetrics().ptScores` (`src/hooks/useStudentExternalMetrics.ts:52`).
- Effort: medium. Easiest now is imported scores plus localStorage runs. A real trend line needs PT runs written to the table.

**2. Questions answered this week: EXISTS**
- `daily_stats.questions_answered` per `class_id` and `date` (types.ts:1585). `useStudentAnalytics` only reads today's row (`src/hooks/useStudentAnalytics.ts:53`).
- Effort: low. One query for the last 7 days, summed.

**3. Sessions left on the booking pass: EXISTS**
- Table `booking_passes` has `total_credits` and `used_credits` (types.ts:1131).
- Home already calls `useStudentBooking()` (`useClassroomHome.ts:176`), which returns `pass.credits_remaining` (`src/hooks/useStudentBooking.ts:9`).
- Effort: trivial. Pass it through `homeModel`.

**4. Days until test date: EXISTS**
- `profiles.test_date` is set per student, null when undecided (types.ts:3225). It's written during onboarding (`OrientationCard.tsx:342`) and in `PrepSection.tsx:213`.
- Home already has a `daysUntil()` helper (`useClassroomHome.ts:123`).
- Effort: low. One `profiles` select.

**5. Goal score and starting score: PARTIAL**
- Onboarding stores ranges, not numbers: `target_score_band` and `last_score_band` in the `profiles.onboarding` JSON (`src/lib/onboarding.ts:94-100`). The ranges are listed at :13, e.g. `156_160`. Either can also be "not sure" or "not taken".
- Effort: low to medium. Use the middle of each range, or add number columns for exact scores. The starting score could instead fall back to the first PT score.

**6. Accuracy by question type: EXISTS**
- The RPC `attempt_accuracy_rollup(p_class_id)` returns `qtype`, `correct` and `total` (types.ts:5821). The Analytics page uses it (`src/pages/Analytics.tsx:55`) together with `computeAnalyticsFromRollup` (`src/lib/analyticsRollup.ts:39`).
- There's also a cached copy in `profiles.by_qtype_json`, and raw rows in `attempts.qtype` and `correct` (types.ts:594).
- Effort: low. Reuse the RPC, sort by accuracy, and set a minimum number of questions per type.

**7. Days studied this month, and session dates: EXISTS / PARTIAL**
- Study days: rows in `daily_stats` where `questions_answered &gt; 0` for the month. `attempts.timestamp_iso` could also be used.
- Tutoring session dates: `useStudentBooking().bookings[].start_at`. The edge function returns the last 30, past and future (`supabase/functions/student-booking/index.ts:108-115`).
- Admin-logged sessions are in `student_sessions`, but only admins can read them (`supabase/migrations/20260424020000_create_student_sessions.sql:26`).
- Effort: low for the calendar. Session dates are already on Home through the booking hook.

The only real schema or data work is item 1 (saving PT runs to the table) and exact numbers for item 5. Everything else is a query plus mapping in `homeModel`.</result>

## assistant

The data check is back, and I've saved it to the brainstorm file. It comes from reading the code, not from running queries. Most of the Home numbers already exist; two don't in the form the design needs.

**Ready now (just a query and some wiring):**
- **Questions this week:** summed from the daily stats table.
- **Sessions left:** Home already loads the student's booking pass.
- **Days to test:** each student's test date is stored on their profile. It's blank until they set one.
- **Weakest question types:** the same per-type accuracy the Analytics page uses.
- **Calendar:** days with any answered questions, plus their booked session dates.

**Gaps:**
- **Latest PT score and the trend line:** there's a table for practice test results, but the app never saves to it. Tests taken in the app are stored only in the student's own browser, so they're lost on another device. Scores imported from other platforms are saved properly.
- **Goal dial:** onboarding saves score ranges (e.g. 156 to 160), not exact numbers, and a student can pick "not sure".

**Round 3, the last one**

**Q1 - Practice test scores**: Should the app start saving practice test results to the database so Home can show the latest score and a trend? The other options are to show imported scores only, or to drop the PT card.

Recommended: save them to the database. Tests are already being taken; the results just aren't kept anywhere Home can read. It's the only new data work, so it can ship as a second step after the redesign.

---

**Q2 - Goal dial**: Use the middle of the ranges students already chose, or ask each student for an exact goal score?

Recommended: add an exact goal score field, filled in from the middle of their range to start, which the student can change. Hide the dial for anyone who picked "not sure".

---

**Q3 - Empty cards**: What should a new student with no data see in each card?

Recommended: one line saying what fills the card, plus one action button. For example: "Take your first practice test to see your trend" with a Start button.

Once you answer these, I'll write the build plan: the redesign first, then saving practice test scores.

## assistant

I've paused the design work until we've settled more. Two facts that shape the questions below:

- **Notifications:** the app already has a notifications system (graded work, new notes, session bundles). It's separate from your messages. "One place" means one inbox that shows both.
- **Section results:** every section a student finishes is saved with its first score, blind review score, how many they fixed in blind review, time taken, and accuracy by question type.

**Round 4**

**Q1 - What "section results" should show**: Which one comes first?
- (a) The last 3 to 5 sections as cards: PT and section number, score, blind review score, time.
- (b) One trend over time: section score and blind review score as two lines.
- (c) Only the most recent section, in depth: score, blind review, and the question types missed.

Recommended: (a) with the newest card larger, showing the question types missed. A student can see where they stand and what to fix without leaving Home.

---

**Q2 - Which sections count**: Only full timed sections, or also shorter drills and sections you assign?

Recommended: full sections only, both LR and RC, including the ones you assign. Drills stay out; you said to leave recent drills off Home.

---

**Q3 - "One question at a time for security"**: What are you protecting against?
- (a) Students copying or screenshotting a whole set at once.
- (b) Students seeing questions ahead and not doing them honestly.
- (c) Keeping focus: one question, then the next.

This decides the rules. For example: can a student go back to an earlier question? Can they see the list of what's coming?

Recommended: I need your answer here. If it's (a) or (b), the rule is no question list, no jumping ahead, and going back only to questions already answered.

---

**Q4 - Assigned homework**: Should work you assign follow the same one-question-at-a-time rule as Smart Drill?

Recommended: yes, same rule, so students learn one way of working.

---

**Q5 - Where the inbox lives**: A panel on Home, a bell at the top of every Classroom page, or both?

Recommended: both. The bell opens the same inbox from anywhere, and on Home it shows open as a panel. There's one list, not two copies.

---

**Q6 - What goes in the inbox**: Tick what belongs: your messages, new assignments, graded sections, session reminders, booking changes, new notes and recaps.

Recommended: all six. Your messages stay pinned at the top, because they matter most.

---

**Q7 - The fastest actions**: When a student opens Home, which 3 actions should be one click away?
- Open a specific question (PT, section, question number)
- Continue assigned work
- Smart Drill
- Start a timed section
- Review wrong answers

Recommended: continue assigned work, Smart Drill, and start a timed section. Opening a specific question stays in the search bar at the top.

---

**Q8 - What leaves Home**: The current page has the week strip and the next session card. My earlier design added a month calendar. Which stay?

Recommended: keep the next session, fold the week strip into it as a small row, and drop the month calendar. It adds weight without helping anyone practice.

Saved. So the performance area adapts. It shows whatever view best fits what this student has been doing and what you've assigned, not one fixed chart.

Some views it could pick from. Each one runs on data the app already saves:

- **Question types:** accuracy and trend per type, for students doing a lot of Smart Drill.
- **Sections:** the last few section scores and their blind review scores, for students doing timed sections.
- **Bootcamp progress:** how far through the current bootcamp, and how they did on the checks, for students in one.
- **Assignment results:** how they did on the work you assigned this week.
- **Wrong-answer patterns:** the mistakes they repeat most, from the Wrong Answer Journal.
- **Pace:** types they get right but too slowly.
- **Confidence gaps:** questions they were sure of but got wrong.

**The new Classroom Home (student view first)**

1. **Top:** the search bar for opening any question, a bell for the inbox, and the greeting.
2. **Three one-click actions:** continue assigned work, Smart Drill, and start a timed section.
3. **Performance area:** one main insight with two or three smaller ones under it.

8. **Look:** light and warm, with dark cards for emphasis. Numbers count up and cards settle in when the page loads. All of it turns off for students whose system asks for reduced motion.
9. **Empty states:** a new student sees one line explaining the card and one button to start. You didn't answer this one, so I'm using my recommendation.
10. **Your teacher view:** follows the same design after the student version.

## 1. FLAWS

The design direction is implementable, but the plan needs corrections to its data contracts, launch behavior, and inbox model. These findings are from repository code; deployed schema and student rendering were not verified. No files were modified.

1. **“Smart Drill filtered to a type” is not an existing launch contract.** Adaptive Smart Drill accepts no type/count parameters; filtered practice is a separate launch kind. Implementing the proposal literally either ignores the filter or changes Smart Drill, which is explicitly out of scope. See [drillLaunch.ts:94](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/drillLaunch.ts:94).  
   **Fix:** Launch type/count selections through `kind: 'filter'`, optionally with the existing smart presentation; keep adaptive Smart Drill as its own action.

2. **The all-practice data gap is confirmed, not merely an open question.** `attempts` covers the LR runner; RC records separately, and bootcamp exercises have separate stores. An active RC student could otherwise receive an empty or misleading performance area. See [sectionFreshness.ts:16](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sectionFreshness.ts:16), [rcProgress.ts:151](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/rc/api/rcProgress.ts:151), and [supabase-progress.ts:78](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/topics/abstract/lib/supabase-progress.ts:78).  
   **Fix:** Add a source-to-lens matrix before implementation, with source-specific adapters and explicit coverage labels; do not present LR accuracy as all-practice accuracy.

3. **Several proposed inputs are not available in the named shape.** The assignment feed exposes status and display copy, not structured scores or completion timestamps; `trap_pattern` belongs to journal notes, not `wrong_answer_journal`; sections have legacy score/time fallbacks already handled by a reader. See [useAssignedTodoFeed.ts:16](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useAssignedTodoFeed.ts:16), [wajNotesService.ts:29](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/wajNotesService.ts:29), and [sectionHistory.ts:25](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sectionHistory.ts:25).  
   **Fix:** Name the actual readers and joins per lens; never parse subtitle text for scores or treat manual completion as demonstrated mastery.

4. **The rollup cannot produce the specified 30-day metric.** Its buckets are current seven days, previous seven days, and everything older; the last bucket cannot be trimmed to 30 days. It also counts attempt rows, not distinct questions. See [rollup migration:47](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/supabase/migrations/20260710031524_bf477c7f-e3a5-4717-93b8-a4e2c72c69ca.sql:47).  
   **Fix:** Add a bounded Home-specific RPC with explicit repeat/review rules and distinct-question sample counts, preserving the existing Analytics contract.

5. **The selection rules rank activity volume, not usefulness.** Twenty question attempts and one completed section have incomparable units; the same section may drive several redundant cards. “Minimum eight” also leaves undefined what happens with fewer than three eligible lenses, an inactive student, or an unsupported pinned lens.  
   **Fix:** Define normalized per-lens scores, deterministic ties, redundancy suppression, sample rules, and a pinned-insight fallback; show fewer cards when evidence is insufficient.

6. **The inbox already duplicates events across its two sources.** Assignment and recap writers create both a chat message authored as Joshua and a notification. A simple merge displays the same event twice, and sender-based pinning elevates automated assignment notices as personal messages. See [classroomNotify.ts:87](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/classroomNotify.ts:87) and [sessionBundleNotify.ts:48](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/lib/sessionBundleNotify.ts:48).  
   **Fix:** Introduce an event identity and explicit message origin for new writes,...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

- New, separately named hook `useHomeFeed()` (existing `useInbox` untouched), on TanStack Query keys shared by Home panel and bell.
- **Event identity**: additive nullable `event_key text` on BOTH `notifications` and individual `messages`, plus `origin text check in ('human','system')` on individual messages (not threads). Generated writers (`classroomNotify.ts:87`, `sessionBundleNotify.ts:48`, new writers) compute one deterministic key per event (e.g. `assignment:<id>:assigned`) and insert both rows with it; partial unique indexes `(recipient, event_key) where event_key is not null` on each table make retries no-ops (`on conflict do nothing`). Human-typed messages get `origin='human'`, no key.
- **Protection**: extend the notifications immutability trigger (`20260506124235_...sql:120`) to cover `event_key`/`origin`, and the same on messages; tests prove a student cannot change either.
- **One canonical visible-event model** (computed server-side in one RPC `home_feed(p_limit, p_before)`): an event with a key appears once (message wins, notification suppressed); legacy rows without keys use the 5-minute same-link heuristic inside the same RPC. The badge count comes from the same RPC (`unread_count` over visible events), so counts never include hidden rows.
- **Read**: opening a visible event marks every row sharing its key read (message participant timestamp + notification boolean) in one RPC `home_feed_mark_read(event)`. Mark-all-read applies to visible events only.
- **Thread rows**: the preview is Joshua's latest unread human message when one exists, else the latest message; unread = any unread human message from Joshua in the thread, not only the latest (fixes `useInbox.ts:189` style latest-only check for this feed).

## FLAWS

Two material problems remain in the revised fixes. The other addressed findings can stay closed.

1. **The proposed RC dispatch opens one passage, not a section.** The finder’s RC link supplies `passage=<id>`. The player then creates a session with exactly that passage and `DEFAULT_CONFIG`, so this route does not preserve the selected full section or timer. This is confirmed by [RcPlayer.tsx:130](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/rc/pages/RcPlayer.tsx:130). The player separately supports multiple passage IDs and explicit configuration at [RcPlayer.tsx:141](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/features/rc/pages/RcPlayer.tsx:141).  
   **Fix:** Specify an RC section launch that resolves all passages in section order, creates a session with the intended timing configuration, and navigates using its session ID.

2. **Event-specific reads cannot safely use a conversation-wide read timestamp.** Suppose Joshua sends an unread human message, then an assignment event arrives. Opening the assignment and advancing `last_read_at` also marks the earlier human message read, even though the student only opened the assignment. That contradicts the revised promise to mark only the selected event’s rows read. The existing mechanism is explicitly conversation-wide: [useInbox.ts:247](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/src/hooks/useInbox.ts:247).  
   **Fix:** Keep generated-event read state separate from the conversation watermark; advance that watermark only when the conversation is read, and use event acknowledgements—including a fallback for message-only delivery—to synchronize generated duplicates.

## IDEAS

No additional ideas this round. The plan already contains enough useful additions; resolving these two behavioral contracts is more valuable than expanding scope.

VERDICT: REVISE
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

Codex never said APPROVED. We used all 3 rounds and it returned REVISE each time. There's no open disagreement, though: I accepted every problem it found and changed the plan for each one. Its last two fixes went in after the final round, so Codex hasn't checked them.

**Files:**
- Plan: [2026-09-23-classroom-home-PLAN.md](../../brainstorms/2026-09-23-classroom-home-PLAN.md)
- Full record of the review: [2026-09-23-classroom-home-PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md](../../brainstorms/2026-09-23-classroom-home-PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md)

The reviewer was gpt-6-astra at medium effort, read-only, in one session across all 3 rounds.

**What the review caught.** My first plan would have shipped these problems:
- **Practice by question type:** Smart Drill can't be limited to one question type. The plan now uses the app's existing filtered-drill launch and leaves Smart Drill alone.
- **"All practice" data:** RC and bootcamp results are saved separately from LR. Each insight now says exactly which work it counts, so an LR-only number is never presented as everything.
- **Inbox duplicates:** assignments and recaps would have shown up twice. Each event now gets one ID shared by its message and its notification, plus rules so the unread badge and "mark as read" match what's on screen.
- **Unread messages:** opening an assignment would have marked your earlier unread message as read. Conversation reads and event reads are now tracked separately.
- **Section data:** the existing section reader turns missing old data into zeros and today's date. Home now uses its own reader.
- **RC sections:** the RC link in the search bar opens one passage, not a whole section. The plan adds a real RC section launch that keeps the chosen timer.
- **Reminders:** nothing sends session reminders today. The plan adds a scheduled job for them, which needs a Lovable deploy.
- **Current Home features:** a checklist makes sure section briefings, report-card tracking, access gates and the week strip all survive the redesign.

**What your "specific question / dropdown" idea became.** The top bar is a practice launcher with three tabs, opened from any Classroom page with Ctrl+K:
- **Question:** PrepTest, Section and Question dropdowns. The button changes as you pick: "Start section" once a section is chosen, "Open question" once a question is.
- **Section:** each section shows how many of its questions the student hasn't answered yet ("24 of 25 unanswered"), with a fresh-only switch. It uses their timer setting, including accommodations.
- **By type:** pick question types, how many and difficulty, with an option for questions they haven't seen. If there aren't enough new ones, it says so before starting.

**Other additions from Codex:**
- Every insight gets a "See the questions behind this" link showing the exact questions it's based on.
- Home reuses the section report's existing diagnosis instead of inventing a second one.
- A diagnosis can open a drill already set to that exact skill.

**Three decisions only you can make:**
1. **Shelves in the launcher:** add "Flagged" and "Needs review" lists, with Needs review sorted by the review date each student set for themselves?
2. **Two buttons on a weak area:** "Review these" (go back over the ones they missed) next to "Try new ones"?
3. **Session reminders:** do you want the app to send them, 24 hours and 1 hour before each session? It's new work and needs a Lovable deploy.

After that: should Codex build phase 1 (`/codex-build`), should I build it, or do you want to stop at the plan? Phase 1 is the layout and restyle using data that already exists, with no database changes.

The Claude Design canvas is still on the older design. My last attempt to read it failed and I haven't retried. I'll bring it in line with this plan before we build.

**v2 Classroom Home** (student view, sample numbers in brackets):
- **Top:** the practice launcher bar with Question, Section and By type tags and a Ctrl K hint, plus the inbox bell with an unread count.
- **Greeting:** the date line, "Good morning, [First name]," and one line saying what's due and whether you left a note.
- **Three actions:**
  - **Continue assigned work** is the dark card, so it stands out first.
  - **Smart Drill**.
  - **Start a timed section**.
- **Main insight:** a dark card, for example "Necessary Assumption is where your next points are." It shows:
  - which work it counts ("LR practice, last 30 days, 22 questions")
  - a 4-week trend
  - "Try new ones" and "Review these" buttons
  - a "See the questions behind this" link
  - a "Chosen for you" label, which would read differently when you've pinned a view
- **Three smaller insights:** their last section (score, blind review, how many they recovered), Wrong Answer Journal reviews due, and progress on work you assigned.
- **Inbox:** your latest message pinned at the top, then new assignment, section graded, session reminder, recap posted and booking moved.
- **Next session:** a dark card with Join, Reschedule, and sessions left on their pass.
- **Next seven days:** a copy of today's strip with nothing changed.

**v2 Practice launcher.** Click the tabs in Play mode:
- **Question:** PrepTest, Section and Question dropdowns, with "Open question 14" and "Start all of Section 2." Below them sit the Needs review list, sorted by each student's own due date and showing their next step, and the Flagged list.
- **Section:** a timer choice (standard, 52.5, 70 or untimed) and a fresh-only switch. Each section shows how many of its questions are new. RC shows "Not tracked yet."
- **By type:** all 12 question types as clickable chips, how many, difficulty, and new-only. If too few new questions are left, it says so, with a link to include answered ones.

@@ -393,3 +397,19 @@ export function buildWeek(

   });
 }
+
+/** Reading matter and completed Report Cards are not work to continue. */
+export function pickContinueWork(rows: HomeRow[], access: HomeAccess, isAdmin: boolean): HomeRow | null {
+  if (isAdmin) return null;
+  const deadline = (row: HomeRow) => {
+    if (!row.dueAt) return Infinity;
+    const time = (parseLocalDay(row.dueAt) ?? new Date(row.dueAt)).getTime();
+    return Number.isFinite(time) ? time : Infinity;
+  };
+  return rows.filter((row) =>
+    !["recap", "note"].includes(row.kind) &&
+    !row.key.startsWith("report:") &&
+    (row.kind !== "activity" || access.activities) &&
+    (row.tone !== "drill" || access.drill)
+  ).sort((a, b) => deadline(a) - deadline(b))[0] ?? null;
+}

I checked the new Home on screen, fixed two layout problems I found, and opened a PR: https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/133. It isn't merged and isn't live on lsatprep.study.

**What I checked, signed in as you on the local server:**
- Desktop (1440px), tablet (900px) and phone (375px): no sideways scroll at any width. Tablet and phone switch to one column.
- Dark mode works.

**What I fixed:**
- **Empty left column:** your account has no assigned work, so the left side was blank. Now, when there's nothing to put there, the inbox, next session and Read, Recall, Colour cards spread across the page instead. Students with nothing assigned would have hit this too.
- **Blank action slot:** with only two action cards showing, a third of the row was empty. The cards now share the full row however many there are.

After these fixes, typecheck is still clean and all 107 tests pass.

**What I couldn't verify:**
- **The student view:** I've only seen your teacher view on screen. There's no "view as student" option, so the student version is covered by tests only. The Continue card and the assignment card only appear for students. To see those, sign in with a student test account.
- **Six console errors:** the browser logged six failed requests (status 400). The request log didn't keep enough history to show which calls they were. They can't come from this change, because phase 1 makes no new requests. I haven't checked whether they also happen on the live site.

**Left out on purpose:** `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts`. The dev server rewrites that file automatically; it isn't part of this change.

**Other PRs:** #132 (the sideways scrollbar fix) and #133 are both waiting for you to merge. After merging, the site still needs a Lovable deploy to go live on lsatprep.study.

Next up is phase 2, the performance area that picks what to show each student.

## assistant

Saved: from now on gpt-6-astra designs, writes specs and reviews, and gpt-6-sol at low effort does all building. I'll state the model before every launch. Sol is in your Codex model list.

**Why it's too uniform: my spec, not the model.** Astra built exactly what I asked for. The problem is what I asked for.

1. **I banned the parts that make your references look good.** The phase 1 spec said "restyle using only the data Home already has": no numbers, no charts, no insight card. With no data, Codex could only restyle the existing cards, so you got a row of equal white boxes.
2. **My own v2 design was already too even.** Three identical action cards, three identical small insight cards, and side cards all the same size. Your references almost never repeat equal boxes.
3. **Your account shows the emptiest version.** You have no assigned work, so the page drops to its simplest state.

**What your references do that ours doesn't:**

| Reference | What it does | Ours today |
|---|---|---|
| Homies Lab | One large tinted top panel holds the greeting, 4 big numbers and a gauge, all in one piece | Greeting floats on its own, and actions are separate equal boxes |
| Be.run | One large visual (the overlapping bubbles) takes two thirds of the row, next to one dark card | No visuals at all, only text cards |
| WeatherWise | Huge thin numbers, and a single curve across the week carries the page | No big numbers and no shape |
| Be.run habits | A list where each row has its own segmented progress bar | Plain list rows |
| All of them | Mixed sizes: one clearly dominant element, then smaller ones around it | Everything roughly the same weight |

**How I'd fix it:**
1. **Redesign first (v3 on the canvas), with astra critiquing the design, not building it:**
   - One large tinted top panel with the greeting, 3 or 4 big numbers, and the practice actions as small rounded buttons inside it, instead of three separate boxes.
   - Below it, a mixed-size grid: one large visual insight taking two thirds of the width (for example question types drawn as shapes or a curve, not text), next to the dark Next session card.
   - Then a list with a segmented progress bar per question type, like Be.run's habits list.
   - The week strip stays exactly as it is.
2. **Build the data at the same time as the visuals.** The reference look needs real numbers, which means pulling phase 2 (the insight numbers) forward into the same step as the redesign. Otherwise we ship pretty boxes with nothing in them again.
3. **Then gpt-6-sol builds it at low effort, and I review.**

PR #133 is still open and not merged. I'd leave it unmerged and replace it with the v3 build.

**Should I start v3 on the canvas now?** Also tell me whether you're fine with pulling the insight data (phase 2) into the same build, since without it the new visuals would have nothing to show.

**VERDICT:** No: v3 borrows reference 6’s bubbles, pill navigation, and segmented bars, but lacks its disciplined proportions and clear focal point. Based on all six images and v3’s source, the oversized greeting, competing numbers, and crowded performance graphic make it feel assembled rather than art-directed.

**Six changes, ranked by impact**

1. **Make performance the centerpiece.** Replace the large greeting enclosure with a 96–120px header: 36px greeting left, question search right. Place the assignment in a 96px horizontal band beneath it. Keep the 8/4 performance/session split below; leave “Next seven days” exactly unchanged.

2. **Replace the overlapping bubble arrangement.** The 300px bubble starts at x330 while the text extends to roughly x372; their regions overlap. Allocate separate 38% narrative / 62% visualization columns. Use ranked horizontal bars with full category labels, missed/attempted counts, and explicitly labeled accuracy. Highlight the selected category in orange; clicking it updates the explanation and practice actions. Adapt categories to the student’s actual work.

3. **Establish one numerical hierarchy.** The 64px greeting, three 60px metrics, 54px accuracy, and 72px session time all demand attention. Use 48px for the selected performance result, 40px for session time, and 28px for weekly questions/test countdown. Remove the duplicate reviews count from the header; retain the actionable review row.

4. **Reduce the surface treatment.** Keep warm ivory, white, and charcoal, but remove the radial orange glows and saturated peach hero gradient. Reserve orange for the primary action and selected data. Use 24px panel radii, 12px row radii, and pills only for controls. This creates hierarchy without making every container ornamental.

5. **Make supporting data credible.** Replace ten-segment accuracy bars with continuous 6px tracks; 58% should not appear as 60%. Add attempt counts and label changes “pp vs previous 30 days.” Replace the last-section miniature columns with two labeled horizontal bars: “Timed 19/25” and “Blind review 22/25.” Use 12–13px sans-serif metadata instead of tiny, widely spaced monospace.

6. **Clarify the actions.** Replace the bell with a labeled “Inbox · 3” control beside search. Rename “Try new ones” to “Practice Necessary Assumption”; label the assignment action “Continue.” Keep Smart Drill and Timed section together. Show “Session details” until joining is available.

**Generic elements to remove**

“Chosen for you,” “Your points are hiding,” decorative status-dot halos, pervasive italic serif emphasis, and glowing spheres. They advertise personalization without explaining it.

**Real-data risks and fixes**

- **One type/few attempts:** show one compact result with its sample size; suppress rankings and trend claims.
- **No practice:** replace analytics with a short first-practice invitation.
- **No session/assignment:** collapse those panels; offer booking or practice without empty card shells.
- **No test date/reviews/messages:** omit countdown; use compact truthful states.
- **Long names/mobile:** replace fixed 1440×1460 dimensions with fluid width and automatic height; stack panels below 1024px.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

A metacognitive confidence calibration (MCC) tool to help ...
  URL: https://journals.physiology.org/doi/full/10.1152/advan.00156.2021
  ## Abstract
This result revealed overconfidence bias in novice students regarding the correctness of their wrong responses. This study demonstrated the feasibility of using a metacognitive confidence calibration tool to assist novice students in learning safely to make deliberate diagnostic reasoning and decisions on patient care in real time during complex simulations while observing objectively their levels of psychological confidence against patient outcomes.

## INTRODUCTION
To develop a strong metacognitive, critical reasoning capacity, medical students need to both _1_) revise their confidence level in every decision, or learning response, in the context of the relevant piece of training in a progression of expertise development and _2_) be able to learn productively from mistakes in different case scenarios, in practice, while not harming patients.

## MATERIALS AND METHODS
### Rationale for Development of the MCC Tool
Although overconfidence bias may not be easily eliminated given its unconscious entrenchment, support for the deliberate practice of diagnostic reasoning and for the deliberate calibration of one’s overconfidence in diagnostic reasoning against patient outcomes may provide learners with better means and enough feedback for the development of appropriate self-efficacy and reflective expertise.

## RESULTS
### Student Accuracy Findings
Those students also displayed inappropriate metacognitive tracking of their reasoning and level of performance, Such _z_ scores provide a novel way to illuminate student performance and support student learning with personalized feedback on zones of confidence-accuracy for improving metacognitive understanding of one’s hot cognition and its performative outcomes in the diagnosis learning context ...

# How Does WHOOP Recovery Work?

By WHOOP

March 30, 2022

[Share on Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/sharer/sharer.php?u=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.whoop.com%2Fus%2Fen%2Fthelocker%2Fhow-does-whoop-recovery-work-101%2F "Share on Facebook")[Share on Twitter](https://twitter.com/intent/tweet?text=How%20Does%20WHOOP%20Recovery%20Work%3F&url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.whoop.com%2Fus%2Fen%2Fthelocker%2Fhow-does-whoop-recovery-work-101%2F "Share on Twitter")

![](https://images.ctfassets.net/rbzqg6pelgqa/ruIw9Z1x8067nr7ovnTvE/67e018f8ffd6f47c36cc99a16771746f/How-does-WHOOP-recovery-work-HEADER-1800x1000__1_.jpg?fm=webp&q=25&w=3840)

Recovery 101: Everything you need to know about how WHOOP measures your body’s readiness to perform.

Below we’ll dive into Recovery, what it means, how it’s calculated, what you can do to improve it, the relationship between Recovery and Strain, plus how to figure out which choices and behaviors impact your recovery the most.

## What is WHOOP Recovery?

WHOOP recovery is a daily measure of how prepared your body is to perform, no matter what it is that you do. It quantifies how your body is adapting to various stressors–for everything from training for elite athletic competition, to even just working from home in your sweats. After you get up each morning WHOOP calculates your recovery as a percentage, and categorizes it as green, yellow, or re

![](https://images.ctfassets.net/rbzqg6pelgqa/2Ily7LHAyCbEVqOor7BUDt/3318a5359e15a592b8da1dce1dc9b4cf/Recovery-Dials-1-768x363.png?fm=webp&q=25&w=1920)

## WHOOP Recovery is a percentage color-coded as green, yellow or red.

**GREEN (67-99%):** You are well recovered and primed to perform. Whether it’s at home, at work, or in the gym, your body is signaling that it can handle a strenuous day. **YELLOW (34-66%):** Your body is maintaining and ready to take on moderate amounts of strain. **RED (1-33%):** Rest is likely what your body needs. It is working hard to recover, potentially from overtraining, sickness, stress

## How Does WHOOP Calculate Recovery?

WHOOP incorporates several key physiological metrics into your daily recovery: **Heart Rate Variability (HRV):** Literally the variance in time between your heart beats, HRV is a benchmark of your autonomic nervous system that is very useful for evaluating physical fitness. To learn more, check out our [_Ultimate Guide to HRV_](https://www.whoop.com/us/en/thelocker/heart-rate-variability-hrv/). **

![](https://images.ctfassets.net/rbzqg6pelgqa/3wYhQu8tdevI6cE1FE82VT/a1f2e3b895729dcbc43d2fb468b346b4/How-does-WHOOP-recovery-wor-INLINE-1000x1000-1__1_.jpg?fm=webp&q=25&w=2048)

HRV, Resting heart rate, Sleep & Respiratory Rate are all Factored into your daily recovery. [Blood oxygen level](https://www.whoop.com/us/en/thelocker/measure-normal-blood-oxygen-levels/) and [skin temperature](https://www.whoop.com/us/en/thelocker/what-is-skin-temperature-and-why-should-you-monitor-it/) are included in your recovery as well and can help provide a better understanding of when you

## Recovery & Strain

How recovered you are each morning determines your capability to take on [strain](https://www.whoop.com/us/en/thelocker/how-does-whoop-strain-work-101/) that day–the better your recovery, the more strain your body is prepared for.

![](https://images.ctfassets.net/rbzqg6pelgqa/1746IxeV9WZEamrPM3IdFm/12e21fecba58229ffb1fe5de6362a836/How-does-WHOOP-recovery-wor-INLINE-1000x1000-2.jpg?fm=webp&q=25&w=2048)

Your Recovery dictates how much strain your body is ready to handle. Based on your recovery, WHOOP recommends an optimal strain each day. It’s a suggested amount intended to allow you to maintain fitness while still enabling your body to adequately recover (go above and you are “overreaching,” stay below and you are “restoring”).

![](https://images.ctfassets.net/rbzqg6pe...

Codex is still researching; it has started saving Firecrawl results to its folder. I'll be notified when it finishes.

**My top 5, saved before seeing Codex's list:**
1. **Today's session.** Instead of a menu of options, the page gives each student a short plan for the day: "Today: about 35 minutes", with reviews due, the assignment, and a few Smart Drill questions on their weakest type, all under one Start button. This follows Whoop, which recommends a day's workload based on your recovery instead of offering a menu.
2. **Speed or understanding?** A verdict under the last-section tile. If blind review scores well above the timed score, the student knows the material and needs speed, so practice timed. If there's little gap, the problem is understanding, so review first. It's 7Sage's own rule, but no dashboard works it out for the student.
3. **Sure but wrong.** A small chart of confidence against correctness: "You were sure on 9 questions you missed", with a Review button. The app already records confidence on every question and has never shown it.
4. **The coach on the page.**
   - Your pinned note sits on the student's insight card, in your words.
   - An "Ask" button on any insight sends you the evidence behind it.
   - The day before a session, a "Bring to Thursday" list collects their flagged and sure-but-wrong questions, and you see it too.
   - Self-serve prep companies can't copy this.
5. **Craft you can feel.**
   - Home appears instantly from the last visit instead of loading.
   - Placeholder shapes match the real layout instead of spinners.
   - Numbers count up only on the first visit of the day.
   - Opening a card animates into the drill.
   - Keyboard shortcuts.

**Blind spots I found:**
- The page doesn't change as test day approaches: build, then timed sections and review, then taper.
- Meaning shown by color alone, which fails for color-blind students.
- Numbers with no "what it counts" or "last updated".
- No real phone layout.
- You can't see exactly what a student sees.

Once Codex finishes, each of us will grade the other's list without having seen it before. We'll argue it out over a couple of rounds, then I'll bring you the combined ranking and a grill-me round on the finalists.

# Five ways to make v3 a better learning dashboard

2026-09-23 · Independent research; no other researcher consulted.

**My view:** preserve v3’s visual character and make every prominent element connect evidence to a useful action. The reference images support hierarchy, atmosphere, and varied scale; they do not establish learning effectiveness. All five recommendations sharpen existing cards. Keep the warm palette, dark anchors, one inbox, fixed-rule insights, coach pinning, and the seven-day strip unchanged.

Evidence below supports principles or product precedents, not proven LSAT U outcomes. Paths are relative to `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu/`. Sizes, thresholds, and motion timings are proposed specifications. Effort includes missing integration work.

## 1. Prescribe the practice, not just the question type

**Student sees:** Within the existing 420px main insight, reserve a 64–88px action block: “Rebuild the assumption test” → “Open the lesson.” Show a short, fixed explanation tied to the saved finding. A fundamentals finding routes to a mapped bootcamp lesson; a process finding to a guided drill; a reading finding to Read, Recall, Colour; a test-taking finding to a section briefing. Keep the existing alternative practice/review links and top actions. The action block crossfades in 150ms; no automatic movement while reading. Without a supported mapping, show “Review the diagnosis.” Coach-pinned state names Joshua explicitly.

**Why world-class:** The dashboard makes an instructional decision, rather than leaving the student to translate a chart. [Ericsson et al.](https://graphics8.nytimes.com/images/blogs/freakonomics/pdf/DeliberatePractice(PsychologicalReview).pdf) describe deliberate practice as structured work aimed at particular improvements. This extends the plan’s diagnosis-to-filter link to different learning activities.

**Existing foundation:** `src/lib/sectionAnalysis.ts` already contains categories, prescriptions, process families, and supporting questions; `src/lib/sectionHistorySave.ts` saves `analysis_json`. Launches: `src/lib/drillLaunch.ts`; recall activities: `src/features/activity-centre/api/types.ts`. **New:** a coach-reviewed diagnosis-to-resource registry; never infer a lesson from free text.

**Effort: M. Risk:** an inaccurate diagnosis can prescribe the wrong work. Expose the finding, preserve alternatives, and let Joshua’s pin win.

## 2. Turn “revisit” into a learning loop with an ending

**Student sees:** Expand v3’s amber wrong-answer strip to approximately 112px: “Revisit 3 questions” with three quiet stages: **Recall → Reattempt → Try a new question**. Launch the existing player; explanations stay concealed until the student attempts the question. After the due reviews, offer one fresh, matching question as a separate step. Return Home to “3 revisited · fresh check: 1/1,” never “mastered.” In-progress work resumes; no due items yields “Nothing scheduled for review.” A saved completion produces one 180ms check transition, no celebration.

**Why world-class:** It adds retrieval and transfer checking to the already-planned due queue. [Roediger and Karpicke](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1111/j.1467-9280.2006.01693.x) found better delayed recall after testing than restudying prose. [Khan Academy](https://support.khanacademy.org/hc/en-us/articles/360037127892-What-are-Mastery-Challenges-in-course-mastery) revisits previously practised skills according to elapsed time and mastery. The fresh-question check is my proposed LSAT adaptation, not a validated mastery test.

**Existing foundation:** `src/lib/wajNotesService.ts` supplies due dates, next actions, and lessons; `src/hooks/useJournalEntries.ts` supplies entries; `src/lib/drillLaunch.ts` supports question lists and filters. **New:** durable loop/outcome records and exposure-aware fresh selection. Claude’s strongest contribution is making coaching visible inside the work. Its weakest is turning blind-review score differences into a confident diagnosis.

**Grades for Claude’s five ideas**

1. **Today’s session — B:** A finishable sequence could reduce indecision, but WHOOP is an analogy rather than learning evidence, and prescribing “Smart Drill on Necessary Assumption” conflicts with LSAT U’s distinction between adaptive Smart Drill and filtered practice.
2. **Speed or understanding? — D:** Relevant question, unjustified verdict: a score gap alone cannot establish the cause of mistakes, the thresholds lack support, and LSAT U already has a richer question-level diagnosis engine.
3. **Sure but wrong — B+:** Useful, well matched to existing data, and directionally supported by calibration research, but “overconfidence is the most common study failure” is unsupported and the proposal leaves rating definitions and collection timing unresolved.
4. **The coach is on the page — A−:** Signed pin notes and evidence-attached questions fit paid tutoring exceptionally well, but automatic agenda collection needs student control and the claimed competitive exclusivity is unsubstantiated.
5. **Craft you can feel — B−:** Fast, stable loading and keyboard access matter, but the evidence does not establish that count-ups or shared-element transitions improve studying, and “instant” promises more than caching guarantees.

**Claude’s blind spots**

- **Test-day phases — C:** Calendar context is real; automatic “14 days = timed sections” and “3 days = no new material” are unsupported prescriptions that could override the student’s actual needs. Make this coach-controlled.
- **Color-only meaning — B:** Valid audit requirement, overstated as a discovery: v3 already includes numeric changes, type labels, and counts. Check every state rather than assume the entire design relies on colour.
- **Freshness and trust — A−:** Update timestamps and stale-state handling are real omissions; denominators and coverage labels are already explicit in the plan.
- **Phone first — B+:** A deliberate phone composition matters, but responsive/container-width work is already planned. The useful addition is prioritising what appears before the first scroll.
- **Joshua’s mirror — A:** A strong addition: Joshua needs the student’s actual selected insight, evidence, and pin state, not merely a similarly styled teacher dashboard.

**What I would steal—and reject**

I would steal **the signed note on a pinned insight, plus Ask with attached evidence**. It gives Joshua’s judgment a precise location and lets students ask about the actual finding without reconstructing it in a message.

I reject **the blind-review verdict as written**. “You know more than the clock lets you show” overstates what the score difference proves. Additional time, reconsideration, and question-specific errors need examination. Three sections reduce noise; they do not validate the causal inference.

**Where we overlap**

- **Practice prescription:** Mine is better grounded: route a saved diagnosis to the appropriate learning activity. Claude’s sequence is better at defining a stopping point. Combine those strengths without forcing assignments into step two.
- **Confidence calibration:** Mine is stronger on evidence and data integrity: explicit buckets, missing ratings, initial attempts, and cautious interpretation. Claude’s headline is shorter and more immediately actionable.
- **Coaching:** Claude wins on bringing Joshua into the insight itself; mine wins on deliberate agenda selection, draft/share states, and workload boundaries.
- **Craft and trust:** Claude gives performance deserved prominence. Mine better addresses stale evidence, misleading charts, and layout stability. Neither list demonstrates student outcomes yet.

**Merged TOP 7, ranked**

1. **Diagnosis-to-action:** Turn the main insight into a justified next step: lesson, guided practice, recall activity, review, or timed s...

I accept moving the finishable sequence to third. I still put diagnosis-to-action first.

**Coaching is the strongest differentiator; diagnosis is the strongest daily function.** A student opening Home between sessions needs a defensible next action. A signed note, contextual question, or prepared agenda strengthens that decision, but does not necessarily supply it. Coaching deserves first place if student research shows that uncertainty about Joshua’s direction is the dominant problem. We have not established that. “No competitor can offer it” is also unnecessary to the argument; contextual coaching can be valuable without being exclusive.

**The sequence is useful, but it is not a prerequisite.** The existing main insight can host the diagnosis action; the existing review strip can host the review loop. Neither requires another card. Joshua rejected visual uniformity, which is not the same as rejecting separate functions.

I move the sequence up because it can answer both “Where do I start?” and “When am I finished?” Its value depends on restraint: optional, resumable, no forced review-before-assignment order, and no automatic reshuffling on focus. Start with one recommended task and disclose the remaining sequence beneath it. A three-step daily prescription should earn its complexity through student use.

**Final ranked seven I can sign off on**

1. **Diagnosis-to-action:** Make the main insight recommend the right learning activity, supported by question-level evidence and a small, Joshua-reviewed resource registry.
2. **Coaching attached to the work:** Combine signed pin notes, Ask with evidence, and one student-selected session brief with explicit draft/shared states.
3. **A finishable practice sequence:** Offer an optional, persistent path through appropriate work, with a clear endpoint and preserved direct-launch actions.
4. **Review followed by a fresh check:** Separate successful reattempts from performance on unfamiliar questions; never equate either with mastery.
5. **Confidence calibration:** Identify useful review candidates from verified pre-feedback ratings, with explicit coverage and missing-data handling.
6. **Truthful visual evidence:** Preserve v3’s expressive charts while correcting scale, denominators, comparisons, and claims.
7. **Fast, stable, accessible Home:** Treat this as a release requirement across all six ideas, not work deferred until seventh.

**Unresolved: first place**

- **Claude:** Coaching first because it delivers the paid tutoring relationship.
- **Codex:** Diagnosis first because it guides independent practice; coaching strengthens and can override it.

**Unresolved: the sequence’s role**

- **Claude:** Today is the organising host for diagnosis and review.
- **Codex:** Today is an optional route through those functions; their existing placements must remain independently useful.
